That was a little over two years ago. It just sort of started then and hasn't really stopped.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to, or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm getting older, so I might not be as fast as I used to be, but... no, I move around just fine. And I definitely don't have any trouble sitting still. I know people are expecting me to be there, but I just can't seem to get going anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the activities you used to do, like choir and quilting, when you do manage to do them?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't actually made it to choir or picked up my quilting in a good long while, so... I couldn't tell you if I still like them. I just don't have the energy to try.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. Like I said, I mostly just sit with the TV on these days. It passes the time, I guess... but I wouldn't say I feel much pleasure from it. I just don't feel much of anything about it either way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I don't have the energy for much, but... no, I haven't thought about anything like that. I don't want to hurt myself or anything. I just... I just sit.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any trouble with that. I sleep pretty well, mostly. I mean, I might doze off in my chair during the day since I'm just sitting there with the TV on, but... no, at night I sleep just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: No, my appetite is about the same. I eat when I'm supposed to, I guess. I don't really think about it much, but I'm not eating any more or less than usual. Just regular meals.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been a while now. I guess... this has been building up since I retired from the school office. That was a little over two years ago. I mean, I'm getting older, so I might not be as fast as I used to be, but... no, I move around just fine. And I definitely don't have any trouble sitting still. I mean... I don't have anything to feel guilty about, really. I haven't done anything wrong. And worthless... no, I don't feel that way. I just live my life quiet-like since I retired, so... no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't really step on the scale much, but... my clothes all still fit me the same as they always have. So, I think my weight has stayed pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing things more slowly than you used to, even when you do get up and do something?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, like I mentioned a minute ago, I know I'm getting older, so maybe I'm a little slower than when I was in my twenties... but day to day? No. When I get up to make a cup of coffee or wash a dish, I just do it. I haven't noticed it taking me any longer than it usually does.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel sad. I mean... I have my days, like anyone else, I suppose. But most of the time? No, I'm just... fine. I don't sit around feeling down or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. That's... that's kind of what brings me in. I just don't have the push I used to have for things. Like choir, or my quilting, or even just visiting folks. Most days, I just sit at home with the TV on low. I know people are expecting me, but I just... I don't go. I don't seem to care to.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I sleep alright most nights, so my body isn't worn out or anything. It's not that I'm physically tired. Like I was just saying, it's more that I just don't have the urge to get up and go. But no, I wouldn't say I feel tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or doing nothing more often than you used to, not because you want to, but because you just can't seem to get started on anything?\n\nPatient: Well... no, I wouldn't put it that way. It's not that I'm trying to do things and just can't get started. \n\nLike I was saying, I just don't have the interest. I sit with the TV on because I'm fine just sitting there. It's not like I want to be doing my quilting and I'm stuck in the chair, unable to move. I just... I look at it and I don't care to do it. So I don't.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself looking forward to things that are coming up?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I guess it just goes back to what I was saying a minute ago. That's really the whole reason I came in... I just don't have that push anymore. Even when I know something is on the calendar, like choir or visiting folks, and I know they're expecting me to be there... I don't get excited for it. I don't look forward to it at all. I just end up staying home with the TV on low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're fidgety or restless, or do you have an inner feeling of jitteriness?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel jittery or restless. I'm actually pretty still most of the time, just sitting in my chair. I don't feel fidgety at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I don't really get on a scale or anything, but... my clothes all fit exactly the same. So I haven't noticed losing any weight.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.     "additional_requirements": ["Onset after significant life change (retirement) confirmed", "Loss of interest in previously enjoyed activities confirmed"]
  }
}
```
